\left|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\left\{\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{v_{i}-u_{i}}{2RT}\right\}\right|\leq C\mathcal{U}(\|\nabla_{x}[\rho,u,T]\|_{W^{|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|,\infty}})\mathbf{M}^{\frac{3}{8}}.

From the above estimates and by repeating the similar computations as for deriving
[
55
, Lemma 10, pp. 327]
again, one sees that all of
G_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime}}^{i}
(1\leq i\leq 6)
can be bounded by the second part of the right hand side of (
2.16
).
This ends the proof of Lemma
2.5
.
∎

Our last lemma in this section is concerned with some estimates related to the difference operator
\mathcal{L}_{d}.

Lemma 2.6
.

Let
\rho(t,x),u(t,x)
and
T(t,x)
satisfy (
1.29
) or (
1.31
), and suppose both (
1.32
) and (
1.33
) are valid. Let the weight function
w_{i},0\leq i\leq s,
be defined in (
1.24
), then it holds that

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}_{d}[h],w_{|\alpha|}^{2}\partial^{\alpha}h\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\bar{\epsilon}\big\|wh\big\|_{H^{|\alpha|}_{D}}^{2}.

(2.17)

Moreover, if we set
\sqrt{\mathbf{M}}f=\sqrt{\mu}h
, then it holds that

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}_{d}h,\partial^{\alpha}h\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\bar{\epsilon}\Big(\big\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})h\|_{H^{|\alpha|}_{D}}^{2}+\big\|f\|_{H^{|\alpha|}_{D}}^{2}\Big)

(2.18)

and

\displaystyle\big(\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}[h],w_{|\alpha|}^{2}\partial^{\alpha}h\big)\geq

\displaystyle\delta\big|w_{|\alpha|}\partial^{\alpha}h\big|_{D}^{2}-C\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\leq\alpha}|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(2.19)

Proof.

By (
1.18
), we rewrite

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}_{d}f=\Gamma\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}-\mu}{\sqrt{\mu}},f\right)+\Gamma\left(f,\frac{\mathbf{M}-\mu}{\sqrt{\mu}}\right).

(2.20)

On the other hand, from (
1.32
) and (
1.33
), it follows that

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha}\mathbf{M}|\lesssim\bar{\epsilon}\mu^{5/6}

(2.21)

holds for any
\alpha>0.
Moreover (
1.32
) and (
1.33
) together with mean value theorem gives

\displaystyle|\mathbf{M}-\mu|=\left|[\nabla_{[\rho,u,T]}\mathbf{M}](\bar{\rho},\bar{u},\bar{T})\cdot(\rho-1,u,T-T_{c})\right|\lesssim\bar{\epsilon}\mu^{5/6},

(2.22)

where
\bar{\rho}\in(\min\{\rho,1\},\max\{\rho,1\})
,
\bar{u}_{i}\in(\min\{u_{i},0\},\max\{0,u_{i}\})
with
1\leq i\leq 3
and
\bar{T}\in(T_{c},T).

Thus, (
2.21
) and (
2.22
) yield

\Big|\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{M}-\mu)\Big]\Big|\lesssim\bar{\epsilon}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}},

(2.23)

and then (
2.17
) follows from (
2.20
), (
2.23
) and (
2.15
) in Lemma
2.5
.
Once (
2.17
) is proved, (
2.18
) follows from

\displaystyle h=\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}f

(2.24)

and the fact that

\displaystyle\left\|\partial^{\alpha}{\bf P}\left[h^{\varepsilon}\right]\right\|^{2}_{D}\lesssim\left\|\partial^{\alpha}{\bf P}\left[h^{\varepsilon}\right]\right\|^{2}\lesssim\left\|f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{H^{|\alpha|}}.

(2.25)

It remains now to prove (
2.19
). To this end, we have by applying
[
55
, Lemma 9, pp. 323]
with
q=1/(8\ln(\mathrm{e}+t))
that

\displaystyle\big(\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}h,w_{|\alpha|}^{2}\partial^{\alpha}h\big)\geq

\displaystyle\delta\big|w_{|\alpha|}\partial^{\alpha}h\big|_{D}^{2}-C(\eta)\big|\partial^{\alpha}h\big|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{C}(\eta))}.

This together with (
2.24
) gives (
2.19
). This completes the proof of Lemma
2.6
.
∎

3.
Hilbert expansion for the Landau equation

In this section, we intend to justify the validity of the Hilbert expansion (
1.6
) for the Cauchy problem (
1.1
) and (
1.4
) of the Landau equation (
1.1
). To this end, the main task is to determine the coefficients
F_{n}

(0\leq n\leq 2k-1)
and to solve the remainder
F_{R}^{\varepsilon}
via the equation (
1.11
) together with the initial datum

f^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)=f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)=\varepsilon^{-k}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\{F^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)-\sum\limits_{n=0}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{k}F_{n,0}(x,v)\right\}.

Note that
h^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)=h^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)=\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{M}_{[\rho_{0},u_{0},T_{0}]}^{\frac{1}{2}}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v).

3.1.
The coefficients

Recall that
F_{0}=\mathbf{M}_{[\rho,u,T]}
with
[\rho,u,T]
being given by Proposition (
1.1
),
thus to obtain the estimates of the coefficients
F_{n}
for all
0\leq n\leq 2k-1
in the expansion (
1.6
),
it suffices to show the following lemma.

Lemma 3.1
.

Under the condition (
1.36
), for
q\in(0,1)
and
\ell\geq 0
, there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N+4k-2n+2}\left|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}\left(\frac{F_{n}}{\sqrt{\mathbf{M}}}\right)\right|\leq C\langle v\rangle^{-\ell+n+|\beta|}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{q}{2}},\ 1\leq n\leq 2k-1.

(3.1)

Proof.

The proof is much similar to that of Lemma
4.1
, we thus omit the details for brevity.
∎

Remark 3.1
.

The main purpose of introducing the velocity derivatives in (
3.1
) is to obtain the
L^{\infty}
bound of the coefficients
F_{n}(t,x,v)
only, which is further used to prove the nonnegativity of the solutions
F^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)
, see the details given in
[
22
, Appendix, pp. 680]
. Similar consideration will also be presented in Lemma
4.1
in Section
4
.

3.2.
The remainder

Once the coefficients
F_{n}
(0\leq n\leq 2k-1)
are given as above, we can now turn to determine the remainder
f^{\varepsilon}

through the following Cauchy problem

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{rll}&\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)f^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}[f^{\varepsilon}]=-f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{k-1}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})\\[5.69054pt]
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\Big[\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},f^{\varepsilon})+\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})\Big]+\mathcal{P}_{0},\\[5.69054pt]
&f^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)=f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v).\end{array}\right.

As we pointed out in the introduction, in order to deduce the estimates of
f^{\varepsilon}
, it is necessary to consider
h^{\varepsilon}
which is connected to
f^{\varepsilon}
by

\sqrt{\mathbf{M}}f^{\varepsilon}=\sqrt{\mu}h^{\varepsilon}

and satisfies

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{rll}&\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)h^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}[h^{\varepsilon}]=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}_{d}[h^{\varepsilon}]+\varepsilon^{k-1}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon})\\[5.69054pt]
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\Gamma(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},h^{\varepsilon})+\mathcal{C}(h^{\varepsilon},\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})]+\mathcal{P}_{1},\\[5.69054pt]
&h^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)=h^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)=\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\mathbf{M}}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v).\end{array}\right.

In fact, the existence in arbitrary time interval
[0,t_{e}]
of (
3.2
) and (
3.2
) follows from a local existence in a small time interval and the
a priori
energy estimate as well as a continuation argument. Here, for the sake of simplicity, we only show the
a priori
energy estimate under the
a priori
energy assumption

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq t_{e}}\mathcal{E}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(3.10)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)=\sum_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i}\left(\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|^{2}\right).

Our first result in this direction is on the energy estimates of
f^{\varepsilon}
, which can be stated as in the following proposition.

Proposition 3.1
.

Assume that
f^{\varepsilon}
and
h^{\varepsilon}
are smooth solutions of the system (
3.2
) and (
3.2
) in time interval
[0,t_{e}]
and satisfy (
3.10
), then it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i}\|\nabla^{i}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\delta\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i-1}\|\nabla^{i}_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}

(3.11)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{0}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{1}_{D}}^{2}+\epsilon_{0}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(\frac{-\epsilon_{0}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{2k+3+i}.

To prove Proposition
3.1
, the trouble term is the velocity cubic growth
\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Our strategy to overcome this difficulty is to use
\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}
to absorb such a growth. To illustrate this and make our presentation clear, we conclude the corresponding estimates in the following lemma.

Lemma 3.2
.

Assume that
f^{\varepsilon}
and
h^{\varepsilon}
are smooth solutions of the system (
3.2
) and (
3.2
) in time interval
[0,t_{e}]
, it holds for each
i=0,1,2
that

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big(f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big),\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\epsilon_{0}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon^{(i+1-j)}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{j}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}

(3.12)

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}.

Proof.

First of all, from (
1.29
), it follows that

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big(f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big),\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{U}(\|\nabla_{x}[\rho,u,T]\|_{W^{j,\infty}})\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2+2j}\nabla_{x}^{j}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|

(3.13)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\epsilon_{0}\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2+2(i-j)}\nabla_{x}^{j}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|.

In order to eliminate the velocity growth in the right hand side of (
3.2
), we divide the domain of the
v-
integration
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
into
\varepsilon-
dependent low velocity part and high velocity part, so that the microscopic and macroscopic component of the low velocity part can be bounded by
\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\partial_{x}^{i}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}
and
\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}
respectively, while the high velocity part can be bounded by
\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}
due to the choice of
T_{c}
in the global Maxwellian
\mu
.
More specifically, by (
1.32
), one has for suitably small
\epsilon_{0}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{\rho}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\frac{T}{T_{c}}\Big)^{3/2}\exp\left(-\frac{(T-T_{c})|v|^{2}}{4RTT_{c}}+\frac{v\cdot u}{2RT}+\frac{|u|^{2}}{4RT}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{(T-T_{c})|v|^{2}}{8RTT_{c}}\right)\lesssim C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{0}|v|^{2}}{16RT^{2}_{c}}\right),

which further implies that

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right|=

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big)\right|\lesssim C_{\epsilon_{0}}\sum_{j=0}^{i}\left|\nabla_{x}^{j}h^{\varepsilon}\right|\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{0}|v|^{2}}{16RT^{2}_{c}}\right).

(3.14)

Thus we get from (
3.14
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2+2(i-j)}\nabla_{x}^{j}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\langle v\rangle}^{4(i+1-j)}\leq\varepsilon^{-(i+1-j)}}{\langle v\rangle}^{3+4(i-j)}\left|\nabla_{x}^{j}f^{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\,dvdx

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\langle v\rangle}^{4(i+1-j)}\geq\varepsilon^{-(i+1-j)}}{\langle v\rangle}^{3+4(i-j)}\left|\nabla_{x}^{j}f^{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\,dvdx

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2+2(i-j)}\nabla_{x}^{j}{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{(i+1-j)}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{j}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}

(3.15)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{i}C_{\epsilon_{0}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\langle v\rangle}^{4(i+1-j)}\geq\varepsilon^{-(i+1-j)}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{0}|v|^{2}}{8T^{2}_{c}}\right)|h^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,dvdx

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sum_{j=0}^{i}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{(i+1-j)}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{j}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}.

\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|
enjoys the same bound as above. This completes the proof of Lemma
3.2
.
∎

Now we turn to prove Proposition
3.1
with the aid of Lemmas
2.3
,
2.4
and
3.2
.

Proof.

The proof is divided into the following three steps.

Step 1. Zeroth order energy estimate on
f^{\varepsilon}
.
In this step we deduce the zeroth order energy estimate on
f^{\varepsilon}
. In fact, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}\lesssim\,\epsilon_{0}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2k+3}.

(3.16)

To this end, we take the
L^{2}
inner product of
f^{\varepsilon}
and(
1.11
) and use (
2.8
) to get that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{D}

(3.17)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\Big|\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}},f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\big|\big\langle[\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},f^{\varepsilon})+\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})\Big],f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\big|\big\langle\mathcal{P}_{0},f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|.

We now turn to estimate terms on the R.H.S. of (
3.17
) term by term.

From (
3.2
) in Lemma
3.2
, one sees that the first term on the R.H.S. of (
3.17
) can be dominated by

\displaystyle\frac{C\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+C\epsilon_{0}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}.

For the second term on the R.H.S. of (
3.17
), noting that for
k\geq 3
, we can deduce from the
a priori
assumption (
3.10
) that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},

(3.18)

thus we can get by (
2.4
) that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}}|f^{\varepsilon}|_{D}|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]|_{D}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big(\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}\Big)\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}.

For the third term on the R.H.S. of (
3.17
),
we get from (
3.1
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\left|\left\langle\left[\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},f^{\varepsilon})+\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})\right],f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon\big(\|{f^{\varepsilon}}\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}.

For the last term on the R.H.S. of (
3.17
), using Cauchy’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\mathcal{P}_{0},f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j\geq 2k+1\\
2\leq i,j\leq 2k-1\end{subarray}}\varepsilon^{2(i+j-k)}\|F_{i}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}\|F_{j}\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2k+3}.

Finally, substituting the above estimates into (
3.17
) gives (
3.16
).

Step 2. Estimates on the first order derivative of
f^{\varepsilon}
.

In this step, we proceed to derive the estimate of
\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}
. For results in this direction, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\varepsilon\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big)+\delta\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\epsilon_{0}\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\Big)

(3.19)

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(\frac{-\epsilon_{0}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}+\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\right)+\varepsilon^{2k+4}.

To prove (
3.2
), we have by applying
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2)
to (
1.11
) that

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}[f^{\varepsilon}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\Big(f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)+\varepsilon^{k-1}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})

(3.20)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},f^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})\Big]+\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\mathcal{P}_{0}.

Letting
|\alpha|=1
, we take the inner product of (
3.2
) with
\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\nabla_{x}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}[f^{\varepsilon}],\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big(f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big),\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

(3.21)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\big|\big\langle[\nabla_{x}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},f^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})\big],\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\mathcal{P}_{0},\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|.

For the second term on the L.H.S of (
3.2
), it follows from
(
2.11
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\nabla_{x}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}[f^{\varepsilon}],\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{3\delta}{4\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}-C\epsilon_{0}\Big(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big).

We now control the terms on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
). For the first term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
), from (
3.2
) with
i=1
, it follows

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big(f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big),\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\epsilon_{0}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\big(\|\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big).

For the second term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
), we use (
2.4
) and (
3.18
) to obtain

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big(\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|\nabla_{x}{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}\Big)\Big(\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\epsilon_{0}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|\Big)

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{0}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|\Big)\Big(\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\epsilon_{0}\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\Big).

For the third term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
), in view of (
2.4
) and (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\big|\big\langle[\nabla_{x}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},f^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})\big],\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\big(\|{f^{\varepsilon}}\|_{H^{1}}+\|{f^{\varepsilon}}\|_{H^{1}_{D}}\big)\Big(\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|\Lbrack\nabla_{x},{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}\Rbrack[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}\Big)

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{0}\big(\|{f^{\varepsilon}}\|+\|{f^{\varepsilon}}\|_{D}\big)\Big(\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\epsilon_{0}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}.

Similarly, for the last term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
), one gets from (
2.4
) and (
3.1
) that

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\mathcal{P}_{0},\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2k+3}.

Finally, plugging the estimates above into (
3.2
) and multiplying the resulting inequality by
\varepsilon
gives (
3.2
).

Step 3. Estimates on the second order derivative of
f^{\varepsilon}
.

In this step, we intend to derive the energy estimates on
\partial^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}
for
|\alpha|=2
. For results in this direction, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla^{2}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big)+\delta\varepsilon\|\nabla^{2}_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{0}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{1}_{D}}^{2}+\epsilon_{0}\Big(\varepsilon^{2}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\Big)

(3.22)

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(\frac{-\epsilon_{0}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\varepsilon^{2k+5}.

To prove (
3.2
), we first get from (
3.2
) with
|\alpha|=2
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}[f^{\varepsilon}],\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big(f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big),\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

(3.23)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\big|\big\langle[\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},f^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})\big],\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{P}_{0},\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|.

By (
2.11
) and (
1.29
), one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}[f^{\varepsilon}],\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{3\delta}{4\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle-C\epsilon_{0}\Big(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{1}_{D}}^{2}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\Big).

We now turn to estimate the terms on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
) separately. For the first term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
),
we get from (
3.2
) with
i=2
that

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}

\displaystyle\Big(f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big),\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\epsilon_{0}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon^{3}}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}.

For second term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
), we can obtain by using (
2.4
) and (
3.18
) as well as Sobolev’s inequalities that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\Big(|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}|_{D}|f^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}}+|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}}|f^{\varepsilon}|_{D}+|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}}|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}|_{D}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|\Lbrack\nabla_{x}^{2},{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}\Rbrack[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}\Big)

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{0}\varepsilon^{k-1}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big(\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{1}_{D}}+\|{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{1}_{D}}\Big)\Big(\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big(\|\nabla^{2}_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|\nabla^{2}_{x}{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}\Big)\Big(\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\epsilon_{0}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{0}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{1}_{D}}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}\Big)\Big(\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{2}_{D}}^{2}+\epsilon_{0}\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\Big).

Similarly, for the third and fourth term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
), applying
(
2.4
) and (
3.1
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\Big[\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},f^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})\big],\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\big(\|{f^{\varepsilon}}\|_{H^{2}}+\|{f^{\varepsilon}}\|_{H^{2}_{D}}\big)\Big(\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|\Lbrack\nabla_{x}^{2},{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}\Rbrack[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}\Big)

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{0}\big(\|{f^{\varepsilon}}\|_{H^{1}}+\|{f^{\varepsilon}}\|_{H^{1}_{D}}\big)\Big(\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{1}_{D}}^{2}+\epsilon_{0}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}

and

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{P}_{0},\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{D}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\varepsilon^{2k+3}.

Putting all the above estimates into (
3.2
) and multiply the resulting inequality by
\varepsilon^{2}
gives (
3.2
).

Now (
3.11
) follows from (
3.16
), (
3.2
) and (
3.2
), this end the proof of Proposition
3.1
.
∎

To close the estimates established in (
3.11
), we now turn to derive the
H^{2}
estimates of
h^{\varepsilon}
, which is the main content of the following lemma.

Proposition 3.2
.

Assume that
f^{\varepsilon}
and
h^{\varepsilon}
are smooth solutions of the system (
3.2
) and (
3.2
) in time interval
[0,t_{e}]
and satisfy (
3.10
), then it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}

\displaystyle\big(\varepsilon^{i+1}\|\nabla^{i}_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big)+\delta\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i}\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}

(3.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\epsilon_{0}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\varepsilon^{i}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{i-1}_{D}}^{2}+\epsilon_{0}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{2k+3+2i}.

Proof.

The proof of Proposition
3.24
is easier than that of Proposition
3.1
, since the operators we will deal with are perturbations of the global Maxwellian
\mu
. Our proof is divided into two steps.

Step 1. Basic Energy Estimate of
h^{\varepsilon}
.
In this step, we deduce the basic
L^{2}
estimates of
h^{\varepsilon}
. For results in this direction, we have

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\varepsilon\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big)+\delta\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}\lesssim\,\epsilon_{0}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2k+3}.

(3.25)

To prove this, we first take the
L^{2}
inner product with
h^{\varepsilon}
and (
1.16
) and use (
2.7
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{D}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big|\big\langle\mathcal{L}_{d}[h^{\varepsilon}],h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon}),h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

(3.26)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\big|\big\langle[\Gamma(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},h^{\varepsilon})+\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})],h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\big|\big\langle\mathcal{P}_{1},h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|.

For the first term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
), by (
2.18
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big|\big\langle\mathcal{L}_{d}[h^{\varepsilon}],h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon}\big(\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{D}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big).

Next, note that for
k\geq 3
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\|h^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim 1

(3.27)

by (
3.10
), using this and (
2.13
), we see that the second term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
) can be dominated by

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon}),h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|h^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}}|h^{\varepsilon}|_{D}|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]|_{D}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\|h^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big(\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|{{\bf P}}[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}\Big)\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\|{{\bf P}}[h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}\lesssim\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}.

It should be pointed out that we always bound
{{\bf P}}[h^{\varepsilon}]
by
f^{\varepsilon}
so that the
\varepsilon-
singularity of
{{\bf P}}[h^{\varepsilon}]
can be avoid.

For the third term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
), from (
2.13
) and (
3.1
), it follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\big|\big\langle[\Gamma(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},h^{\varepsilon})+\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})],h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon\big(\|{f^{\varepsilon}}\|_{D}^{2}+\|{f^{\varepsilon}}\|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}.

Finally, for the last term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
),
using Cauchy’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\mathcal{P}_{1},h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j\geq 2k+1\\
2\leq i,j\leq 2k-1\end{subarray}}\varepsilon^{2(i+j-k)}\|F_{i}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}\|F_{j}\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2k+3}.

Substituting the above estimates into (
3.2
) and multiplying the resulting inequality by
\varepsilon
give (
3.25
).

Step 2. Higher order energy estimates of
h^{\varepsilon}
.
In this step, we shall deduce the
H^{2}
estimate of
h^{\varepsilon}
, our results in this direction can be summarized as follows

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big)+\varepsilon\delta\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}\lesssim\,\epsilon_{0}\varepsilon\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\epsilon_{0}\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\varepsilon^{2k+5}

(3.28)

and

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\varepsilon^{3}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big)+\varepsilon^{2}\delta\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}\lesssim\,\epsilon_{0}\varepsilon^{2}\Big(\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{D}}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}\Big)+\varepsilon^{2k+6}.

(3.29)

For brevity, we only prove (
3.29
), since (
3.28
) can be shown similarly. To do this,
applying
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}
with
|\alpha|=2
to (
1.16
) and using (
2.7
), one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{D}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{d}[h^{\varepsilon}],\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\nabla^{2}_{x}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon}),\nabla^{2}_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

(3.30)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\big|\big\langle[\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},h^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})],\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{P}_{1},\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|.

We now turn to compute the the R.H.S. of (
3.2
) term by term.
For the first term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
),
in light of (
2.18
), one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{d}[h^{\varepsilon}],\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\varepsilon}\big(\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{D}}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}\big).

For second term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
),
by (
2.25
), (
2.13
), (
3.27
) and Sobolev’s inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon}),\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\Big(|\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}}|h^{\varepsilon}|_{D}|+|h^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}}|\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}|_{D}|+|\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}}|\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}|_{D}\Big)|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}]|_{D}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\|h^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{D}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\|{{\bf P}}[\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}.

Similarly, for the third and fourth term on the R.H.S. of (
3.2
),
by (
2.13
) and (
3.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\big|\big\langle

\displaystyle[\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},h^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})],\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon\big(\|{h^{\varepsilon}}\|_{H^{2}_{D}}^{2}+\|{h^{\varepsilon}}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{1}_{D}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}

and

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{P}_{1},\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2k+3}.

Plugging the above estimates into (
3.2
) and multiplying the resulting inequality by
\varepsilon^{3}
give (
3.29
).

Finally, (
3.24
) follows from (
3.25
), (
3.28
) and (
3.29
), this finishes the proof of Proposition
3.2
.
∎

3.3.
The Proof of the Theorem
1.1

We are now in a position to complete the Proof of Theorem
1.1
. We will only show that the energy estimates (
1.37
) holds and omit the details of proof of the non-negativity of
F^{\varepsilon}
since it is the same as the proof in
[
51
,
52
]
.

To this end, we first get from Propositions
3.1
and
3.2
that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{E}(t)

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i}\Big(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}^{i}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{D}+\|\nabla_{x}^{i}({\bf I}-{\bf P})[h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{D}\Big)

(3.31)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{0}\Big(\varepsilon^{2}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(\frac{-\epsilon_{0}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\varepsilon^{2k+3},

where
\mathcal{E}(t)
is defined in (
1.34
).

Next, by choosing
\varepsilon_{0}>0
sufficiently small such that

C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(\frac{-\epsilon_{0}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon_{0}}}\right)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{3},

then for
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
and
t\in[0,t_{e}]
, we can get from Gronwall’s inequality and (
3.31
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}(s)\,ds\lesssim\mathcal{E}(0)+\varepsilon^{2k+3},\ t\in[0,t_{e}],

where
\mathcal{D}(t)
is given in (
1.35
). The proof of Theorem
1.1
is then complete.

4.
Hilbert expansion for VML system

In this section, we shall justify the validity of the Hilbert expansion (
1.19
) for the VML system (
1.2
).
As discussed in Section
3
, the proof relies on two ingredients, the first one is to determine the coefficients
[F_{n},E_{n},B_{n}]
, which is given by the iteration equations (
1.20
), the other is to prove the wellposedness of the remainder
[f^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon}_{R},B^{\varepsilon}_{R}]
via the equations (
1.22
) with prescribed initial data

\displaystyle f^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)=

\displaystyle f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)=\varepsilon^{-k}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\{F^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)-\sum\limits_{n=0}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{k}F_{n,0}(x,v)\right\},

\displaystyle E_{R}^{\varepsilon}(0,x)=

\displaystyle E^{\varepsilon}_{R,0}(x)=\varepsilon^{-k}\left\{E^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x)-\sum\limits_{n=0}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{k}E_{n,0}(x)\right\},

\displaystyle B_{R}^{\varepsilon}(0,x)=

\displaystyle B^{\varepsilon}_{R,0}(x)=\varepsilon^{-k}\left\{B^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x)-\sum\limits_{n=0}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{k}B_{n,0}(x)\right\}.

Recall that
h^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)=h^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)=\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{M}_{[\rho_{0},u_{0},T_{0}]}^{\frac{1}{2}}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v).

4.1.
The coefficients

This subsection is devoted to determining the coefficients
F_{n}
for all
1\leq n\leq 2k-1
in the expansion (
1.19
).
Our results are the following

Lemma 4.1
.

Under the condition (
1.40
), for
q\in(0,1)
,
\ell\geq 0
and
1\leq n\leq 2k-1
, there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N+2k-2n+2}\left|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}\left(\frac{F_{n}}{\sqrt{\mathbf{M}}}\right)\right|\leq C\epsilon_{1}\langle v\rangle^{-\ell+n+|\beta|}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{q}{2}}(1+t)^{n}

(4.1)

and

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|\leq N+2k-2n+2}|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}[E_{n},B_{n}]|\leq C\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{n}.

(4.2)

Proof.

The main argument applied here is originally used in

[
22
]
for the case of cutoff Boltzmann equation and Landau equation around global Maxwellian.
Setting
F_{n}=\sqrt{\mathbf{M}}f_{n}
and recalling the definition (
1.25
), we write

{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}[f_{n}]=\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\chi_{0}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\frac{1}{\sqrt{R\rho T}}u_{n}^{i}\chi_{i}+\frac{T_{n}}{\sqrt{6\rho}}\chi_{4}.

Next, from (
1.20
), one sees that
f_{n}
satisfies the following iterative equations

\displaystyle L_{\mathbf{M}}f_{1}=-\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\{\partial_{t}\mathbf{M}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbf{M}\}+\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{M},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f_{1}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{1}+f_{1}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}-(E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{1}

\displaystyle-f_{1}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}-\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(E_{1}+v\times B_{1})\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{M}+\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}f_{2}=\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f_{1},f_{1}),

\displaystyle\cdots\cdots\cdots\cdots\cdots,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f_{n}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{n}+f_{n}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}-(E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{n}

\displaystyle-f_{n}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}-\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(E_{n}+v\times B_{n})\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{M}

\displaystyle-{\bf 1}_{n>1}\sum\limits_{i+j=n\atop{i,j\geq 1}}(E_{i}+v\times B_{i})\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{j}-{\bf 1}_{n>1}\sum\limits_{i+j=n\atop{i,j\geq 1}}f_{j}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(E_{i}+v\times B_{i})\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}f_{n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i+j=n+1\atop{i,j\geq 1,i\neq j}}\{\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f_{i},f_{j})+\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f_{j},f_{i})\}+{\bf 1}_{n+1=2\mathbb{Z}}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f_{\frac{n+1}{2}},f_{\frac{n+1}{2}}),

(4.3)

\displaystyle\cdots\cdots\cdots\cdots\cdots,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f_{2k-2}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{2k-2}+f_{2k-2}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}-(E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{2k-2}

\displaystyle-f_{2k-2}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}-\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(E_{2k-2}+v\times B_{2k-2})\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{M}

\displaystyle-\sum\limits_{i+j={2k-2}\atop{i,j\geq 1}}(E_{i}+v\times B_{i})\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{j}-\sum\limits_{i+j={2k-2}\atop{i,j\geq 1}}f_{j}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(E_{i}+v\times B_{i})\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}f_{2k-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i+j=2k-1\atop{i,j\geq 1}}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f_{i},f_{j}),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f_{2k-1}

\displaystyle+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{2k-1}+f_{2k-1}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}-(E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{2k-1}

\displaystyle-f_{2k-1}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(E+v\times B)\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}-\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(E_{2k-1}+v\times B_{2k-1})\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{M}

\displaystyle-\sum\limits_{i+j={2k-1}\atop{i,j\geq 1}}(E_{i}+v\times B_{i})\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{j}-\sum\limits_{i+j={2k-1}\atop{i,j\geq 1}}f_{j}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(E_{i}+v\times B_{i})\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i+j=2k}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f_{i},f_{j}).

To prove (
4.1
) and (
4.2
), our purpose now is to show that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N+4k-2n-2}\left|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-n-|\beta|}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{q}{2}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}f_{n}\right|+\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|\leq N+2k-2n-2}\left|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}[E_{n},B_{n}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{n},

(4.4)

holds for
N\geq 2
.

In fact, (
4.4
) follows from

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N+4k-2n+2}\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-n-|\beta|}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{q^{\prime}}{2}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}f_{n}\right\|_{D}+\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|\leq N+2k-2n+2}\left|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}[E_{n},B_{n}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{n}

(4.5)

with
0<q<q^{\prime}<1
, because for
0<\eta\ll 1
, we have the following estimates

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{q^{\prime}-\eta}{2}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}f_{n}\right\|\leq C\left\|\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{q^{\prime}}{2}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}f_{n}\right\|_{D}

and

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m}\left|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-n-|\beta|}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{q}{2}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}f_{n}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum\limits_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m+4}\left\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}\{\langle v\rangle^{\ell-n-|\beta|}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{q}{2}}f_{n}\}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+C\sum\limits_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m+4\atop{\bar{\alpha}+\bar{\beta}>0}}\left\|\partial_{\bar{\beta}}^{\bar{\alpha}}\{\langle v\rangle^{\ell-n-|\beta|}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{q}{2}}\}\partial_{\beta-\bar{\beta}}^{\alpha-\bar{\alpha}}f_{n}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum\limits_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m+4}\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-n-|\beta|}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{q^{\prime}-\eta}{2}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}f_{n}\right\|^{2}

for
l\geq|\beta|
and
q<q^{\prime}-\eta.

We now verify (
4.5
) by the method of induction. To do so, the macroscopic and microscopic parts of
f_{n}
should be estimated separately.
For the macroscopic component
{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}[f_{n}]
, taking the following velocity moments

\mathbf{M}^{1/2},(v-u)\mathbf{M}^{1/2},\left(\frac{|v-u|^{2}}{RT}-3\right)\mathbf{M}^{1/2}

with
1\leq i,j\leq 3
for the equation (
4.1
), respectively, one has

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\partial_{t}\rho_{n}+\nabla_{x}\cdot u_{n}+\nabla_{x}\cdot(\rho_{n}u)=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}u_{n}+\nabla_{x}[RT(\rho_{n}+2T_{n})]+\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\otimes u_{n})+(\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla_{x}u)\rho_{n}+\nabla_{x}u\cdot u_{n}\\[5.69054pt]
\qquad+\rho_{n}E+u_{n}\times B+\rho_{n}u\times B+\rho E_{n}+\rho u\times B_{n}\\[5.69054pt]
\qquad+\sum\limits_{i+j=n\atop{i,j\geq 1}}(\rho_{i}E_{j}+u_{i}\times B_{j}+\rho_{i}u\times B_{j})\\[5.69054pt]
\quad=-\nabla_{x}\cdot\left(\left[(v-u)\otimes(v-u)-\frac{|v-u|^{2}}{2}\mathbf{I}\right]\mathbf{M}^{1/2},({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{n}]\right),\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}T_{n}+2\nabla_{x}\cdot u_{n}+\nabla_{x}\cdot(uT_{n})+\frac{\partial_{t}T}{T}(3\rho_{n}+T_{n})+\frac{5\nabla_{x}T\cdot u_{n}}{T}+\frac{\nabla_{x}T\cdot u(3\rho_{n}+T_{n})}{T}\\[5.69054pt]
\quad=-\nabla_{x}\cdot\left((v-u)\left(\frac{|v-u|^{2}}{RT}-5\right)\mathbf{M}^{1/2},({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{n}]\right),\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}E_{n}-\nabla_{x}\times B_{n}=u_{n}+\rho_{n}u,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}B_{n}+\nabla_{x}\times E_{n}=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{n}=-\rho_{n},\ \nabla_{x}\cdot B_{n}=0,\end{array}\right.

where
1\leq n\leq 2k-1
and
\mathbf{I}
stands for the unit
3\times 3
matrix.
Then, by standard energy estimates, it follows from (
4.1
) and (
1.31
) that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|\leq m}\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}[\rho_{n},u_{n},T_{n},E_{n},B_{n}]\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|\leq m}\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}[\rho_{n},u_{n},T_{n},E_{n},B_{n}]\|^{2}

\displaystyle+C\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|\leq m+1}\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{n}]\|_{D}\sum\limits_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}[\rho_{n},u_{n},T_{n},E_{n},B_{n}]\|

(4.16)

where
m>0
is finite.

To close our estimate, we now turn to estimate the microscopic part
({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{n}]
.
For
n=1
, in view of the first equation for
\varepsilon^{0}
in (
1.20
), one has for
q^{\prime}\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}L_{\mathbf{M}}[({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{1}]],\langle v\rangle^{2(\ell-1-|\beta|)}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{1}]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\langle\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}\left[\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\{\partial_{t}\mathbf{M}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbf{M}\}\right],\langle v\rangle^{2(\ell-1-|\beta|)}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{1}]\right\rangle,

from this and (
(ii)
) as well as (
1.31
), we get for
\beta>0
that

\displaystyle\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-1-|\beta|}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{1}]\right\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle-\left(\eta+C\epsilon_{1}\right)\sum_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}+|\bar{\alpha}|\leq\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|}\sum_{|\bar{\beta}|=|\beta|}\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-1-|\bar{\beta}|}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\bar{\alpha}_{0}}\partial^{\bar{\alpha}}_{\bar{\beta}}({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{1}]\right\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle-C(\eta)\sum\limits_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\leq\alpha_{0}}\sum_{\bar{\alpha}\leq\alpha}\sum_{|\bar{\beta}|<|\beta|}\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-1-|\bar{\beta}|}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\bar{\alpha}_{0}}\partial^{\bar{\alpha}}_{\bar{\beta}}({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{1}]\right\|^{2}_{D}\leq C\epsilon_{1}^{2},

and for
\beta=0

\displaystyle\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-1}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{1}]\right\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle-C\epsilon_{1}{\bf 1}_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|>0}\sum\limits_{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\leq\alpha_{0},\bar{\alpha}\leq\alpha,|\bar{\alpha}_{0}|+|\bar{\alpha}|<|\alpha_{0}|+|\alpha|}\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-1}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\bar{\alpha}_{0}}\partial^{\bar{\alpha}}({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{1}]\right\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle-C\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-1}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{1}]\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{C}(\eta))}\leq C\epsilon_{1}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{1}]\right\|_{D}^{2}\leq C\epsilon_{1}^{2}.

Putting the above estimates together, we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m+4k-3}\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-1-|\beta|}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{1}]\right\|_{D}^{2}\leq C\epsilon_{1}^{2}.

(4.17)

Thus (
4.17
) and (
4.1
) with
n=1
gives

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m+4k-4}\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-1-|\beta|}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}f_{1}(t)\right\|_{D}+\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|\leq m+4k-4}\left|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}[E_{1},B_{1}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|\leq m+4k-4}\left\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}[\rho_{1},u_{1},T_{1},E_{1},B_{1}](0,x)\right\|+C\epsilon_{1}(1+t).

(4.18)

Now we assume that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m+4k-2n-2}\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-n-|\beta|}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}f_{n}(t)\right\|_{D}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|\leq m+4k-2n-2}\left|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}[E_{n},B_{n}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|\leq m+4k-2n-2}\left\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}[\rho_{n},u_{n},T_{n},E_{n},B_{n}](0,x)\right\|+C\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{n}

holds for
1\leq n\leq 2k-2
, then by repeating the argument used to deduce (
4.17
), one has

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m+4k-2n-3}\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-n-|\beta|-1}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}({\bf I-P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f_{n+1}](t)\right\|_{D}\leq C\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{n},

this together with (
4.1
) yields

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m+4k-2n-4}\left\|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-n-|\beta|-1}\mathbf{M}^{-q^{\prime}/2}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}f_{n+1}(t)\right\|_{D}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{\alpha_{0}+|\alpha|\leq m+4k-2n-4}\left|\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial^{\alpha}[E_{n+1},B_{n+1}]\right|

(4.19)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{n+1}.

Finally, by taking
m=N+4
, (
4.1
) with
0\leq n\leq 2k-2
gives (
4.5
). Thus the proof of Lemma
4.1
is complete.
∎

4.2.
The remainder

In this subsection, we intend to construct the wellposedness of the remainders
f^{\varepsilon},E_{R}^{\varepsilon},B_{R}^{\varepsilon}
and
h^{\varepsilon}
which satisfy the following equations

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}+\frac{\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)}{RT}\cdot\big(v-u\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\frac{v-u}{2RT}f^{\varepsilon}-\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}+\frac{\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}[f^{\varepsilon}]}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}f^{\varepsilon}\Big[\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}\Big]\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{k-1}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\Big[\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},f^{\varepsilon})+\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})\Big]+\varepsilon^{k}\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{k}\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\frac{v-u}{2RT}f^{\varepsilon}

(4.20)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i}\Big[\Big(E_{i}+v\times B_{i}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}+\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i}\Big[\Big(E_{i}+v\times B_{i}\Big)\cdot\frac{\big(v-u\big)}{2RT}f^{\varepsilon}\Big]+\varepsilon^{k}\mathcal{Q}_{0},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}E_{R}^{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}f^{\varepsilon}dv,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\times E_{R}^{\varepsilon}=0,

(4.21)

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{R}^{\varepsilon}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}f^{\varepsilon}dv,\qquad\nabla_{x}\cdot B_{R}^{\varepsilon}=0,

and

\displaystyle\partial_{t}h^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle+v\cdot\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}+\frac{\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)}{RT}\cdot\big(v-u\big)\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{M}

\displaystyle+\frac{E\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}-\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}+\frac{\mathcal{L}[h^{\varepsilon}]}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathcal{L}_{d}[h^{\varepsilon}]}{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon^{k-1}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon})+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}[\Gamma(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},h^{\varepsilon})+\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})]

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon^{k}\frac{E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}

(4.22)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i}\Big[\Big(E_{i}+v\times B_{i}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}+\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{i}\Big]-\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i}\frac{E_{i}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon^{k}\mathcal{Q}_{1},

together with the initial datum

\displaystyle\left[f^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v),E_{R}^{\varepsilon}(0,x),B_{R}^{\varepsilon}(0,x),h^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)\right]=\left[f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v),E^{\varepsilon}_{R,0}(x),B^{\varepsilon}_{R,0}(x),h^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)\right].

(4.23)

The global existence of (
4.20
), (
4.21
), (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) can be established by the local-in-time existence and the
a priori
energy estimate as well as the continuation argument. For the sake of simplicity, we only prove the
a priori
energy estimate (
1.41
) under the
a priori
assumption

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}}}\mathcal{E}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.24)

Due to the presence of the Lorentz force, the energy estimates for the VML system (
1.2
) are much more complicate than those of Section
3
for the Landau equation (
1.1
). Before deducing the
a priori
energy estimate (
1.41
), we first give some key estimates in the following lemmas. The first one is concerned with the velocity growth caused by local Maxwellian
\mathbf{M}_{[\rho,u,T]}
and electromagnetic field without velocity derivatives.

Lemma 4.2
.

Assume that
f^{\varepsilon},E_{R}^{\varepsilon},B_{R}^{\varepsilon}
and
h^{\varepsilon}
are smooth solutions of the Cauchy problem (
4.20
), (
4.21
), (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) for the VML system (
1.2
) and satisfy (
4.24
), then for
i=1,2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big(f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big),4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

(4.25)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{(i+1-j)}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{j}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}\Big),

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\frac{v-u}{T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],2\pi T\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

(4.26)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{(i+1-j)}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{j}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}\Big),

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\frac{(v-u)}{T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],2\pi T\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

(4.27)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{(i+1-j)}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{j}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}\Big),

and for
0\leq t\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla^{i}_{x}\Big[\Big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\Big)\cdot\frac{(v-u)}{T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],2\pi T\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

(4.28)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{(i+1-j)}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{j}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}\Big).

Proof.

We only prove (
4.28
), since (
4.25
), (
4.26
) and (
4.27
) can be proved similarly. To this end, for
0\leq t\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}}
, we get from (
4.4
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\Big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\Big)\cdot\frac{(v-u)}{T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],2\pi T\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}(1+t)^{n}\|{\langle v\rangle}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}\|{\langle v\rangle}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}.

This together with (
3.2
) then gives (
4.28
). This ends the proof of Lemma
4.2
.
∎

The following lemma is devoted to the convection terms also caused by Lorentz force, in particular, the extra velocity derivatives are involved in, which makes the estimates more delicate.

Lemma 4.3
.

Assume that
f^{\varepsilon},E_{R}^{\varepsilon},B_{R}^{\varepsilon}
and
h^{\varepsilon}
are smooth solutions of the Cauchy problem (
4.20
), (
4.21
), (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) for the VML system (
1.2
) and satisfy (
4.24
), then for
i=1,2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\Big[\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i-1}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}

(4.29)

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\Big(\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i-1}}+\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i-1}_{D}}\Big)\Big],

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\Big[\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}

(4.30)

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\Big(\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}_{D}}\Big)\Big],

and

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\Big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}+\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{n}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}\Big[\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i-1}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}

(4.31)

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\Big(\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i-1}}+\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i-1}_{D}}\Big)\Big].

Proof.

We only prove (
4.3
) and (
4.3
), since (
4.3
) can be proved in the same way. By (
1.31
), Sobolev’s inequalities and (
4.24
), one has

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\left[\big(E+v\times B\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\right],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\Big(\|E\|_{W^{i,\infty}}+\|B\|_{W^{i,\infty}}\Big)\Big(\left\|{\langle v\rangle}\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i-1}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\Big)

(4.32)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\Big(\left\|{\langle v\rangle}\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i-1}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\Big)

and

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big)\Big(\left\|{\langle v\rangle}\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\Big)

(4.33)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Big(\left\|{\langle v\rangle}\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\Big).

Then it suffices to control
\|{\langle v\rangle}\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}
with
i=0,1,2
.
To see this, similar to argument to derive (
3.14
), one has

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}|v|^{2}}{8RTT_{c}}\right)\left[{\langle v\rangle}^{2(i-j)+1}|h^{\varepsilon}|+{\langle v\rangle}^{2(i-j)}|\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{j}h^{\varepsilon}|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}|v|^{2}}{12RT^{2}_{c}}\right)\big[|h^{\varepsilon}|+|\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{j}h^{\varepsilon}|\big].

Next, similar to (
3.2
), we use Lemma
2.2
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|{\langle v\rangle}\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\langle v\rangle}^{4}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}{\langle v\rangle}^{2}\left|\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\,dvdx+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\langle v\rangle}^{4}\geq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}{\langle v\rangle}^{2}\left|\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\,dvdx

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}

(4.34)

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\langle v\rangle}^{4}\geq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}|v|^{2}}{8RT^{2}_{c}}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{i}\big(|\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{j}h^{\varepsilon}|^{2}+|\nabla_{x}^{j}h^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\big)\,dvdx

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{D}^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\Big(\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}_{D}}\Big).

Finally, combing (
4.2
) and (
4.2
), (
4.2
) gives the desired estimates. This ends the proof of Lemma
4.3
.
∎

We are ready to deduce the following proposition.

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume that
f^{\varepsilon},E_{R}^{\varepsilon},B_{R}^{\varepsilon}
and
h^{\varepsilon}
are smooth solutions of the Cauchy problem (
4.20
), (
4.21
), (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) for the VML system (
1.2
) and satisfy (
4.24
), then it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i}\Big(\|\sqrt{4\pi RT}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{i}E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{i}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\delta\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i-1}\|\nabla_{x}^{i}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{D}

(4.35)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i}\Big[(1+t)^{-p_{0}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}\Big]\Big(\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\left\|[E_{R}^{\varepsilon},B_{R}^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{H^{i}}^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)(\|h^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}}^{2}+\|h^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}_{D}}^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\varepsilon^{i-1}\Big[(1+t)^{-p_{0}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}\Big]\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i-1}}+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\left\{\varepsilon^{2k+1+i}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k+i}(1+t)^{2k}\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\|\right\}.

Proof.

The proof is divided into three steps.

Step 1. Basic energy estimate of the remainders.
In this step, we derive the
L^{2}
estimates on
[f^{\varepsilon},E_{R}^{\varepsilon},B_{R}^{\varepsilon}]
. Taking the
L^{2}
inner product of (
4.20
) with
4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}
and by applying (
2.8
), one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\|\sqrt{4\pi RT}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)+\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{D}

(4.36)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2}\big|\big\langle\partial_{t}Tf^{\varepsilon},4\pi Rf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\Big|\Big\langle\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot u\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}},4\pi f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big\langle\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot(v-u)f^{\varepsilon},2\pi f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}},4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\big|\big\langle[\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},f^{\varepsilon})+\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})\big],4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\big|\big\langle\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot(v-u)f^{\varepsilon},2\pi f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i}\Big|\Big\langle\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{i},4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i}\Big|\Big\langle\Big(E_{i}+v\times B_{i}\Big)\cdot(v-u)f^{\varepsilon},2\pi f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\big|\big\langle\mathcal{Q}_{0},4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|.

Here we have used the following identity

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}\right)=4\pi\Big\langle v\cdot E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}},f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle

from (
4.21
).

Now we turn to estimate the terms on the R.H.S. of (
4.36
) individually.
By (
1.31
), we see that the first and second terms on the R.H.S. of (
4.36
) can be dominated by

\displaystyle C\|\partial_{t}T\|_{\infty}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+C\|u\|_{\infty}\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\big(\|\nabla_{x}\rho\|_{\infty}+\|u\|_{W^{1,\infty}}+\|\nabla_{x}T\|_{\infty}\big)\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\epsilon_{1}\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big).

In view of Lemma
4.2
and Lemma
4.3
, the 3rd, 4th, 7th, 8th and 9th terms on the R.H.S. of (
4.36
) can be controlled by

\displaystyle C\Big[\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}\Big]\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big).

For the 5th term on the R.H.S. of (
4.36
), one has by the
a priori
assumption (
4.24
) that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.37)

From which, we get from (
2.4
), Sobolev’s inequality and (
4.37
) that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),4\pi RT({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}}|f^{\varepsilon}|_{D}|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]|_{D}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big(\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}\Big)\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon\|{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}\lesssim\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}.

For the 6th term on the R.H.S. of (
4.36
), from (
2.4
) and (
4.1
), it follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}\big|\big\langle[\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i},f^{\varepsilon})+\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{i})\big],4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{i-1}(1+t)^{i}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big(\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}+\|{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}\Big)\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon(1+t)^{2}\|{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{3}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2},

where we also used the fact that
0\leq t\leq\varepsilon^{-1/3}
.

For the last term on the R.H.S. of (
4.36
),
using Cauchy’s inequality, (
4.1
) and (
4.2
), we have

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\mathcal{Q}_{0},4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j\geq 2k+1\\
2\leq i,j\leq 2k-1\end{subarray}}\varepsilon^{2(i+j-k-1)}\|F_{i}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}\|F_{j}\|_{D}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j\geq 2k\\
1\leq i,j\leq 2k-1\end{subarray}}\varepsilon^{i+j-k}\Big(\|E_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|B_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big)\big\|(1+v)\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{v}F_{j}\big\|\|f^{\varepsilon}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{D}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2k+1}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k}(1+t)^{2k}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|.

Substituting the above estimates in (
4.36
) leads to

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\|\sqrt{4\pi RT}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)+\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{D}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\Big[(1+t)^{-p_{0}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{3}}\Big]\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)

(4.38)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2k+1}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k}(1+t)^{2k}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|.

Step 2. Estimates on the first order derivative of the remainders.

In this step, we proceed to deduce the estimate of
\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}E^{\varepsilon}
and
\nabla_{x}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}
. For this, applying
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2)
to (
4.20
) gives

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{t}

\displaystyle+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\Big[\frac{\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)}{RT}\cdot\big(v-u\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big]
